Hello, Doctor. It's very nice to meet you. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I hope I'm not taking up time from someone who might need it more, but... well, my husband really thought it would be best if I came to speak with someone. I hope I explain this right. 

I'm here because... well, it sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud, and I really don't want you to think I'm just a complaining, difficult person, but for nearly two years now, I’ve been sure that our contractor and some of the people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us. They just keep leaving my kitchen half-wrong on purpose.

My husband keeps showing me all these receipts to try and prove everything is fine, and the workers give so many excuses, but... well, I just know what they're doing. Does that make sense? Even with all the paperwork and the endless excuses the workers give us, I can just tell they are quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong. I really hope that doesn't make me sound awful or ungrateful for the work they've done. It's just what I know in my heart is happening. Does that make me sound terrible? I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to deliberately keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you think this is actually physically possible for them to do, or does it involve things that couldn't really happen in the real world?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no, nothing like that. I mean... I really hope you don't think I'm imagining things like magic or science fiction. I know exactly how that would sound, and I promise I'm not making up wild, impossible stories. I'd never want to waste your time with nonsense. \n\nIt's just... well, it's all very ordinary things. Like the contractor ordering the wrong hinges on purpose, or the neighbors whispering to the workers when they park their trucks out on the street. It's all completely possible in the real world, just... just very frustrating for us. I try to be very sensible about it, I really do. I just notice the little everyday things they do to delay the work. Does that answer your question properly? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\n[Last Question]\nBesides the situation with the contractor and your neighbors, have you noticed other people or groups working against you in other areas of your life, or is it mainly just this kitchen situation?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 67 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Besides the situation with the contractor and your neighbors, have you noticed other people or groups working against you in other areas of your life, or is it mainly just this kitchen situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it's just the kitchen. Everyone else in my life is just lovely, really. My friends at church, the people at the grocery store... they're all perfectly fine. I don't want to make a fuss where there isn't one. It's just this one specific situation with the house that's been so... well, so difficult. I hope that makes sense? I really try to get along with everyone else.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I had reviewed everything carefully and found no evidence that the contractor and neighbors were working together, would that be something you could accept, or would you still believe it was happening despite what I found?\n\nPatient: Oh, Doctor... Oh, um... well... I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? 

But... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable.

I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.

I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm accusing anyone unfairly. I know that's not a nice thing to do. But... well, I suppose I do feel like it's a bit intentional. 

I've been dealing with this for almost two years now, and... and I just can't shake the feeling that the contractor and a few of the people on my block are sort of... well, quietly working together to cheat us. Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. 

My husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? He shows me the receipts and the contracts, and the contractor always has some excuse about supply chains or backorders. And I nod and say I understand, because I want to be reasonable. I really do try to be reasonable. But... deep down, no matter what they say or show me, I just know they're doing it on purpose to cause problems for us. 

I know that sounds a little... well, maybe a little paranoid? I hope you don't think I'm a crazy person for saying that. It's just hard to ignore when it's been going on for so long. Does that... does that make sense why I might feel that way?

My husband keeps showing me all these receipts to try and prove everything is fine, and the workers give so many excuses, but... well, I just know what they're doing. Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I'm bringing this up with you.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have there been any times when you doubted whether the contractor and neighbors were actually working together against you, or has it always felt certain to you?\n\nPatient: Oh, I really try to be reasonable, Doctor. I promise I do. I don't want to be one of those stubborn women who just won't listen. When my husband sits me down and goes over all those receipts, I always nod and try to tell myself that maybe I'm just misunderstanding things, because I want to make him happy. \n\nBut... well, deep down? No, I haven't really doubted it. It just feels absolutely certain to me. Even with all the paperwork and the endless excuses the workers give us, I can just tell they are quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong. I really hope that doesn't make me sound awful or ungrateful for the work they've done. It's just what I know in my heart is happening. Does that make me sound terrible? I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to deliberately keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you think this is actually physically possible for them to do, or does it involve things that couldn't really happen in the real world?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no, nothing like that. I mean... I really hope you don't think I'm imagining things like magic or science fiction. I know exactly how that would sound, and I promise I'm not making up wild, impossible stories. I'd never want to waste your time with nonsense. \n\nIt's just... well, it's all very ordinary things. Like the contractor ordering the wrong hinges on purpose, or the neighbors whispering to the workers when they park their trucks out on the street. It's all completely possible in the real world, just... just very frustrating for us. I try to be very sensible about it, I really do. I just notice the little everyday things they do to delay the work. Does that answer your question properly? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Besides the situation with the contractor and your neighbors, have you noticed other people or groups working against you in other areas of your life, or is it mainly just this kitchen situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it's just the kitchen. I promise I don't go around thinking everyone is out to get me. I know how that sounds, and I'd hate for you to think I'm one of those paranoid people who sees shadows everywhere. \n\nIt's really just the contractor and those few neighbors on the block. Everyone else in my life is just lovely, really. My friends at church, the people at the grocery store... they're all perfectly fine. I don't want to make a fuss where there isn't one. It's just this one specific situation with the house that's been so... well, so difficult. I hope that makes sense? I really try to get along with everyone else.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I had reviewed everything carefully and found no evidence that the contractor and neighbors were working together, would that be something you could accept, or would you still believe it was happening despite what I found?\n\nPatient: Oh, Doctor... I really, really want to say yes. I want to be agreeable, and I know you're an expert and you're just trying to help me. I don't want to seem difficult or like I'm questioning your judgment, because I respect you so much. \n\nBut... oh, this is so hard to say without sounding stubborn... even if you told me that, even if you looked at all the paperwork just like my husband does... I think I would still know it's happening. I've just been so sure for nearly two years now that they're quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. I know how awful that makes me sound, like I just won't listen to reason. It's just... no matter how many receipts are shown to me, or how many excuses the workers give, I just can't shake what I know is true. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not the answer you were hoping for. Does that make me a terrible patient? I really am trying my best to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen work isn't finished, do you think they're doing these things specifically to target you and your family, or could there be other reasons they might be doing these things even if they're intentional?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. I hope it doesn't sound too awful of me to say that. I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. It's just the things I actually see with my own two eyes—the truck, the shoddy work, the way they look at me. Just normal things, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you notice the kitchen work isn't finished or see the contractor's truck outside, do you think about what specific meaning or message that might have for you personally, or do you just see it as them being difficult or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to just see it as them being, you know, difficult. Or maybe just not very good at their jobs. I think that's the most sensible way to look at it, don't you? \n\nI don't think there's a... a special message or anything like that. That would sound a bit silly, wouldn't it? Like I was reading too much into things. I really try hard not to do that. It's just... well, it's just the plain fact that the work isn't done and they're dragging their feet. I don't look for hidden meanings or signs or anything. I just want a finished kitchen, like any normal person would. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the work isn't finished, do you think those specific events are happening because they're trying to send you a message, or do you just think they're being careless or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I thought we just talked about that, but maybe I wasn't clear enough. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it right the first time. I really don't want you to think I'm the sort of person who thinks everything is a secret message. \n\nLike I said before, I just think they're being careless. Or maybe they just don't care about the quality of their work. I don't think there's any sort of hidden meaning when the truck is there or when the cabinets are still wrong. It's just... well, it's just bad workmanship, plain and simple. \n\nI know some people might get carried away and think someone is trying to send them a sign, but I promise I don't think that way at all. I'm very grounded, really I am. I just want them to do the job they were hired to do. That's a perfectly normal way to look at it, isn't it? I hope I'm making more sense this time.\n\nDoctor: When the contractor leaves your kitchen half-wrong or parks the truck outside, do you think they're doing it because they specifically want to upset you or cause you problems, or do you think they're just being careless without any particular intention toward you?\n\nPatient: Well... Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nMy husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? I really do try to be reasonable. Like I was reading too much into things. I really try hard not to do that. It's just... well, it's just the plain fact that the work isn't done and they're dragging their feet. I don't look for hidden meanings or signs or anything. I just want a finished kitchen, like any normal person would. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the work isn't finished, do you think those specific events are happening because they're trying to send you a message, or do you just think they're being careless or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I thought we just talked about that, but maybe I wasn't clear enough. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it right the first time. I really don't want you to think I'm the sort of person who thinks everything is a secret message. \n\nLike I said before, I just think they're being careless. Or maybe they just don't care about the quality of their work. I don't think there's any sort of hidden meaning when the truck is there or when the cabinets are still wrong. It's just... well, it's just bad workmanship, plain and simple. \n\nI know some people might get carried away and think someone is trying to send them a sign, but I promise I don't think that way at all. I'm very grounded, really I am. I just want them to do the job they were hired to do. That's a perfectly normal way to look at it, isn't it? I hope I'm making more sense this time.\n\nDoctor: When the contractor leaves your kitchen half-wrong or parks the truck outside, do you think they're doing it because they specifically want to upset you or cause you problems, or do you think they're just being careless without any particular intention toward you?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm accusing anyone unfairly. I know that's not a nice thing to do. But... well, I suppose I do feel like it's a bit intentional. \n\nI've been dealing with this for almost two years now, and... and I just can't shake the feeling that the contractor and a few of the people on my block are sort of... well, quietly working together to cheat us. Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nMy husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? He shows me the receipts and the contracts, and the contractor always has some excuse about supply chains or backorders. And I nod and say I understand, because I want to be reasonable. I really do try to be reasonable. But... deep down, no matter what they say or show me, I just know they're doing it on purpose to cause problems for us. \n\nI know that sounds a little... well, maybe a little paranoid? I hope you don't think I'm a crazy person for saying that. It's just hard to ignore when it's been going on for so long. Does that... does that make sense why I might feel that way?\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you feel like they're doing this specifically to target you and your family personally, or do you think they might be doing the same thing to other customers as well?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 67 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... um, goodness, I really haven't thought about it quite like that. I don't actually know if they do it to other people. I haven't asked around or anything... I mean, it wouldn't be very polite to go prying into other people's business to ask if they're being cheated, would it? 

I certainly don't want to sound self-important, like I think I'm so special that they'd only target me and my family. That would sound terribly arrogant, don't you think? I really try not to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. 

So... well, maybe they are doing it to other customers too? I just don't have any way of knowing what they do on their other jobs. I only know what's happening in my own house. I'm sorry I don't have a better answer for you on that one. I hope it's alright that I'm just not sure? I really am trying my best to give you the right answers.

I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to deliberately keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you think this is actually physically possible for them to do, or does it involve things that couldn't really happen in the real world?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no, nothing like that. I mean... I really hope you don't think I'm imagining things like magic or science fiction. I know exactly how that would sound, and I promise I'm not making up wild, impossible stories. I'd never want to waste your time with nonsense. \n\nIt's just... well, it's all very ordinary things. Like the contractor ordering the wrong hinges on purpose, or the neighbors whispering to the workers when they park their trucks out on the street. It's all completely possible in the real world, just... just very frustrating for us. I try to be very sensible about it, I really do. I just notice the little everyday things they do to delay the work. Does that answer your question properly? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Besides the situation with the contractor and your neighbors, have you noticed other people or groups working against you in other areas of your life, or is it mainly just this kitchen situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it's just the kitchen. I promise I don't go around thinking everyone is out to get me. I know how that sounds, and I'd hate for you to think I'm one of those paranoid people who sees shadows everywhere. \n\nIt's really just the contractor and those few neighbors on the block. Everyone else in my life is just lovely, really. My friends at church, the people at the grocery store... they're all perfectly fine. I don't want to make a fuss where there isn't one. It's just this one specific situation with the house that's been so... well, so difficult. I hope that makes sense? I really try to get along with everyone else.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I had reviewed everything carefully and found no evidence that the contractor and neighbors were working together, would that be something you could accept, or would you still believe it was happening despite what I found?\n\nPatient: Oh, Doctor... I really, really want to say yes. I want to be agreeable, and I know you're an expert and you're just trying to help me. I don't want to seem difficult or like I'm questioning your judgment, because I respect you so much. \n\nBut... oh, this is so hard to say without sounding stubborn... even if you told me that, even if you looked at all the paperwork just like my husband does... I think I would still know it's happening. I've just been so sure for nearly two years now that they're quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. I know how awful that makes me sound, like I just won't listen to reason. It's just... no matter how many receipts are shown to me, or how many excuses the workers give, I just can't shake what I know is true. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not the answer you were hoping for. Does that make me a terrible patient? I really am trying my best to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen work isn't finished, do you think they're doing these things specifically to target you and your family, or could there be other reasons they might be doing these things even if they're intentional?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. I hope it doesn't sound too awful of me to say that. I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. It's just the things I actually see with my own two eyes—the truck, the shoddy work, the way they look at me. Just normal things, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you notice the kitchen work isn't finished or see the contractor's truck outside, do you think about what specific meaning or message that might have for you personally, or do you just see it as them being difficult or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to just see it as them being, you know, difficult. Or maybe just not very good at their jobs. I think that's the most sensible way to look at it, don't you? \n\nI don't think there's a... a special message or anything like that. That would sound a bit silly, wouldn't it? Like I was reading too much into things. I really try hard not to do that. It's just... well, it's just the plain fact that the work isn't done and they're dragging their feet. I don't look for hidden meanings or signs or anything. I just want a finished kitchen, like any normal person would. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the work isn't finished, do you think those specific events are happening because they're trying to send you a message, or do you just think they're being careless or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I thought we just talked about that, but maybe I wasn't clear enough. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it right the first time. I really don't want you to think I'm the sort of person who thinks everything is a secret message. \n\nLike I said before, I just think they're being careless. Or maybe they just don't care about the quality of their work. I don't think there's any sort of hidden meaning when the truck is there or when the cabinets are still wrong. It's just... well, it's just bad workmanship, plain and simple. \n\nI know some people might get carried away and think someone is trying to send them a sign, but I promise I don't think that way at all. I'm very grounded, really I am. I just want them to do the job they were hired to do. That's a perfectly normal way to look at it, isn't it? I hope I'm making more sense this time.\n\nDoctor: When the contractor leaves your kitchen half-wrong or parks the truck outside, do you think they're doing it because they specifically want to upset you or cause you problems, or do you think they're just being careless without any particular intention toward you?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm accusing anyone unfairly. I know that's not a nice thing to do. But... well, I suppose I do feel like it's a bit intentional. \n\nI've been dealing with this for almost two years now, and... and I just can't shake the feeling that the contractor and a few of the people on my block are sort of... well, quietly working together to cheat us. Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nMy husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? He shows me the receipts and the contracts, and the contractor always has some excuse about supply chains or backorders. And I nod and say I understand, because I want to be reasonable. I really do try to be reasonable. But... deep down, no matter what they say or show me, I just know they're doing it on purpose to cause problems for us. \n\nI know that sounds a little... well, maybe a little paranoid? I hope you don't think I'm a crazy person for saying that. It's just hard to ignore when it's been going on for so long. Does that... does that make sense why I might feel that way?\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you feel like they're doing this specifically to target you and your family personally, or do you think they might be doing the same thing to other customers as well?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, goodness, I really haven't thought about it quite like that. I don't actually know if they do it to other people. I haven't asked around or anything... Oh my... I feel terrible, I must not be explaining myself very well today. I'm so sorry if I'm making your job difficult, since I know we just touched on something a bit like this.

I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Besides the situation with the contractor and your neighbors, have you noticed other people or groups working against you in other areas of your life, or is it mainly just this kitchen situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it's just the kitchen. I promise I don't go around thinking everyone is out to get me. I know how that sounds, and I'd hate for you to think I'm one of those paranoid people who sees shadows everywhere. \n\nIt's really just the contractor and those few neighbors on the block. Everyone else in my life is just lovely, really. My friends at church, the people at the grocery store... they're all perfectly fine. I don't want to make a fuss where there isn't one. It's just this one specific situation with the house that's been so... well, so difficult. I hope that makes sense? I really try to get along with everyone else.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I had reviewed everything carefully and found no evidence that the contractor and neighbors were working together, would that be something you could accept, or would you still believe it was happening despite what I found?\n\nPatient: Oh, Doctor... I really, really want to say yes. I want to be agreeable, and I know you're an expert and you're just trying to help me. I don't want to seem difficult or like I'm questioning your judgment, because I respect you so much. \n\nBut... oh, this is so hard to say without sounding stubborn... even if you told me that, even if you looked at all the paperwork just like my husband does... I think I would still know it's happening. I've just been so sure for nearly two years now that they're quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. I know how awful that makes me sound, like I just won't listen to reason. It's just... no matter how many receipts are shown to me, or how many excuses the workers give, I just can't shake what I know is true. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not the answer you were hoping for. Does that make me a terrible patient? I really am trying my best to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen work isn't finished, do you think they're doing these things specifically to target you and your family, or could there be other reasons they might be doing these things even if they're intentional?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. I hope it doesn't sound too awful of me to say that. I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. It's just the things I actually see with my own two eyes—the truck, the shoddy work, the way they look at me. Just normal things, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you notice the kitchen work isn't finished or see the contractor's truck outside, do you think about what specific meaning or message that might have for you personally, or do you just see it as them being difficult or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I don't look for hidden meanings or signs or anything. I just want a finished kitchen, like any normal person would. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the work isn't finished, do you think those specific events are happening because they're trying to send you a message, or do you just think they're being careless or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I thought we just talked about that, but maybe I wasn't clear enough. I hope I'm making more sense this time.\n\nDoctor: When the contractor leaves your kitchen half-wrong or parks the truck outside, do you think they're doing it because they specifically want to upset you or cause you problems, or do you think they're just being careless without any particular intention toward you?\n\nPatient: Well... Oh, um... well, I know it probably sounds a little silly when I say it out loud, and I really hope you don't think poorly of me for thinking this way. I try so hard to give people the benefit of the doubt, I really do.

It's all completely possible in the real world, just... just very frustrating for us. I try to be very sensible about it, I really do. I just notice the little everyday things they do to delay the work. Does that answer your question properly? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Besides the situation with the contractor and your neighbors, have you noticed other people or groups working against you in other areas of your life, or is it mainly just this kitchen situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it's just the kitchen. I promise I don't go around thinking everyone is out to get me. I know how that sounds, and I'd hate for you to think I'm one of those paranoid people who sees shadows everywhere. \n\nIt's really just the contractor and those few neighbors on the block. Everyone else in my life is just lovely, really. My friends at church, the people at the grocery store... they're all perfectly fine. I don't want to make a fuss where there isn't one. It's just this one specific situation with the house that's been so... well, so difficult. I hope that makes sense? I really try to get along with everyone else.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I had reviewed everything carefully and found no evidence that the contractor and neighbors were working together, would that be something you could accept, or would you still believe it was happening despite what I found?\n\nPatient: Oh, Doctor... I really, really want to say yes. I want to be agreeable, and I know you're an expert and you're just trying to help me. I don't want to seem difficult or like I'm questioning your judgment, because I respect you so much. \n\nBut... oh, this is so hard to say without sounding stubborn... even if you told me that, even if you looked at all the paperwork just like my husband does... I think I would still know it's happening. I've just been so sure for nearly two years now that they're quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. I know how awful that makes me sound, like I just won't listen to reason. It's just... no matter how many receipts are shown to me, or how many excuses the workers give, I just can't shake what I know is true. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not the answer you were hoping for. Does that make me a terrible patient? I really am trying my best to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen work isn't finished, do you think they're doing these things specifically to target you and your family, or could there be other reasons they might be doing these things even if they're intentional?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. I hope it doesn't sound too awful of me to say that. I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. It's just the things I actually see with my own two eyes—the truck, the shoddy work, the way they look at me. Just normal things, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you notice the kitchen work isn't finished or see the contractor's truck outside, do you think about what specific meaning or message that might have for you personally, or do you just see it as them being difficult or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to just see it as them being, you know, difficult. Or maybe just not very good at their jobs. I think that's the most sensible way to look at it, don't you? \n\nI don't think there's a... a special message or anything like that. That would sound a bit silly, wouldn't it? Like I was reading too much into things. I really try hard not to do that. It's just... well, it's just the plain fact that the work isn't done and they're dragging their feet. I don't look for hidden meanings or signs or anything. I just want a finished kitchen, like any normal person would. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the work isn't finished, do you think those specific events are happening because they're trying to send you a message, or do you just think they're being careless or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I thought we just talked about that, but maybe I wasn't clear enough. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it right the first time. I really don't want you to think I'm the sort of person who thinks everything is a secret message. \n\nLike I said before, I just think they're being careless. Or maybe they just don't care about the quality of their work. I don't think there's any sort of hidden meaning when the truck is there or when the cabinets are still wrong. It's just... well, it's just bad workmanship, plain and simple. \n\nI know some people might get carried away and think someone is trying to send them a sign, but I promise I don't think that way at all. I'm very grounded, really I am. I just want them to do the job they were hired to do. That's a perfectly normal way to look at it, isn't it? I hope I'm making more sense this time.\n\nDoctor: When the contractor leaves your kitchen half-wrong or parks the truck outside, do you think they're doing it because they specifically want to upset you or cause you problems, or do you think they're just being careless without any particular intention toward you?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm accusing anyone unfairly. I know that's not a nice thing to do. But... well, I suppose I do feel like it's a bit intentional. \n\nI've been dealing with this for almost two years now, and... and I just can't shake the feeling that the contractor and a few of the people on my block are sort of... well, quietly working together to cheat us. Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nMy husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? He shows me the receipts and the contracts, and the contractor always has some excuse about supply chains or backorders. And I nod and say I understand, because I want to be reasonable. I really do try to be reasonable. But... deep down, no matter what they say or show me, I just know they're doing it on purpose to cause problems for us. \n\nI know that sounds a little... well, maybe a little paranoid? I hope you don't think I'm a crazy person for saying that. It's just hard to ignore when it's been going on for so long. Does that... does that make sense why I might feel that way?\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you feel like they're doing this specifically to target you and your family personally, or do you think they might be doing the same thing to other customers as well?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, goodness, I really haven't thought about it quite like that. I don't actually know if they do it to other people. I haven't asked around or anything... I mean, it wouldn't be very polite to go prying into other people's business to ask if they're being cheated, would it? \n\nI certainly don't want to sound self-important, like I think I'm so special that they'd only target me and my family. I really try not to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. \n\nSo... well, maybe they are doing it to other customers too? I really am trying my best to give you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you notice things like the contractor's truck being parked outside or the kitchen work being incomplete, do you think these events are happening by coincidence, or do you feel like they're specifically arranged or timed in a way that's meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... I don't think there's any special timing to it, or any sort of hidden message for me to figure out. I just want the work done, that's all. \n\nDoes that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I really don't want you to think my imagination is running wild or that I'm making up stories in my head. I'm just trying to look at the facts as they are.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen isn't finished, do you ever wonder if those things are somehow meant as a sign or message specifically for you, or do you just see them as the contractor being slow or difficult?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I think we might have just talked about that a little bit? I hope I didn't confuse you earlier. \n\nNo, I don't see them as signs or messages or anything like that. I feel terrible, I must not be explaining myself very well today. I'm so sorry if I'm making your job difficult, since I know we just touched on something a bit like this. \n\nBut, um, no... I don't think they're trying to get a reaction out of me or send any kind of message. It's just about the money for them. Oh, um... well, no, I haven't actually heard them say my name or anything like that out loud. I know that probably makes it sound like I'm just jumping to conclusions, and I really try not to do that, I promise.

I think I would still know it's happening. I've just been so sure for nearly two years now that they're quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. I know how awful that makes me sound, like I just won't listen to reason. It's just... no matter how many receipts are shown to me, or how many excuses the workers give, I just can't shake what I know is true. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's not the answer you were hoping for. Does that make me a terrible patient? I really am trying my best to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen work isn't finished, do you think they're doing these things specifically to target you and your family, or could there be other reasons they might be doing these things even if they're intentional?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well... I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I do feel like it's aimed at us. At me and my husband. I know there could be other reasons, like maybe they're just terribly disorganized, or maybe they're short on supplies... I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. I hope it doesn't sound too awful of me to say that. I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. It's just the things I actually see with my own two eyes—the truck, the shoddy work, the way they look at me. Just normal things, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. They just keep leaving my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nI know it probably sounds terrible of me to say that about people. My husband keeps showing me all these receipts to try and prove everything is fine, and the workers give so many excuses, but... well, I just know what they're doing. Does that make sense? I promise I do. I don't want to be one of those stubborn women who just won't listen. When my husband sits me down and goes over all those receipts, I always nod and try to tell myself that maybe I'm just misunderstanding things, because I want to make him happy. \n\nBut... well, deep down? No, I haven't really doubted it. It just feels absolutely certain to me. Even with all the paperwork and the endless excuses the workers give us, I can just tell they are quietly working together to cheat us and keep the kitchen half-wrong. I really hope that doesn't make me sound awful or ungrateful for the work they've done. It's just what I know in my heart is happening. Does that make me sound terrible? I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to deliberately keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you think this is actually physically possible for them to do, or does it involve things that couldn't really happen in the real world?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no, nothing like that. I mean... I really hope you don't think I'm imagining things like magic or science fiction. I know exactly how that would sound, and I promise I'm not making up wild, impossible stories. I'd never want to waste your time with nonsense. \n\nIt's just... well, it's all very ordinary things. Like the contractor ordering the wrong hinges on purpose, or the neighbors whispering to the workers when they park their trucks out on the street. I just notice the little everyday things they do to delay the work. Does that answer your question properly? It's just this one specific situation with the house that's been so... well, so difficult. I hope that makes sense? Does that make me a terrible patient? I suppose I try to look at it from all angles, I really do. I don't want to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. That would be so self-centered, wouldn't it? \n\nBut... well, when I see the truck just sitting there, or when another week goes by and the cabinets are still crooked... I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you notice the kitchen work isn't finished or see the contractor's truck outside, do you think about what specific meaning or message that might have for you personally, or do you just see it as them being difficult or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I don't actually know if they do it to other people. I haven't asked around or anything... I mean, it wouldn't be very polite to go prying into other people's business to ask if they're being cheated, would it? \n\nI certainly don't want to sound self-important, like I think I'm so special that they'd only target me and my family. That would sound terribly arrogant, don't you think? I really try not to be the sort of person who thinks the whole world revolves around them. \n\nSo... well, maybe they are doing it to other customers too? I just don't have any way of knowing what they do on their other jobs. I only know what's happening in my own house. I'm sorry I don't have a better answer for you on that one. I hope it's alright that I'm just not sure? I really am trying my best to give you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you notice things like the contractor's truck being parked outside or the kitchen work being incomplete, do you think these events are happening by coincidence, or do you feel like they're specifically arranged or timed in a way that's meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... I certainly don't think it's anything like that. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I think everything is some big, dramatic production. I'm just a regular person, you know? \n\nWhen the truck is there, it's just... well, it's just because he drove it there that day. I don't think there's any special timing to it, or any sort of hidden message for me to figure out. I know some people might read into things like that, but I try to be much too practical for that sort of thing. It's just a truck, and an unfinished kitchen. I just want the work done, that's all. \n\nDoes that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I really don't want you to think my imagination is running wild or that I'm making up stories in my head. I'm just trying to look at the facts as they are.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen isn't finished, do you ever wonder if those things are somehow meant as a sign or message specifically for you, or do you just see them as the contractor being slow or difficult?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I must not be explaining myself very well today. I'm so sorry if I'm making your job difficult, since I know we just touched on something a bit like this. \n\nBut, um, no... I don't think they're trying to get a reaction out of me or send any kind of message. That would mean they actually care about my feelings or what I think, and I'm quite sure they don't give me a second thought! \n\nI really just believe it comes down to plain old greed. It's just about the money for them. They're cutting corners and dragging it out for their own financial gain, and they probably couldn't care less whether I notice what they're doing or not, just as long as they get to keep the cash. It's just a simple case of being cheated by dishonest people, nothing more complicated than that. \n\nI hope that's a clearer answer for you? I really do want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. I try to tell myself those things so I don't seem unreasonable. \n\nBut deep down, it just feels so personal. Like they know exactly what they're doing to our home, specifically. I hope it doesn't sound too awful of me to say that. I really don't want you to think I have an inflated sense of importance or anything like that. Is it okay that I feel that way? I just want to be completely honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you're thinking about what the contractor and neighbors are doing, do you ever hear them talking about you or the kitchen when you can't see them, or do you ever see or notice things that seem designed as messages or signs directed at you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't hear voices or anything like that. I really want to make sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not hallucinating or seeing things that aren't there. I know how important it is to be clear about that. \n\nI've never heard them talking when they aren't around, and I don't see secret messages or signs or anything strange like that. It's just the things I actually see with my own two eyes—the truck, the shoddy work, the way they look at me. Just normal things, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary or... or spooky. \n\nI really hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that my mind is perfectly sound when it comes to things like that. I'm just a normal lady dealing with a very frustrating contractor, I promise. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you notice the kitchen work isn't finished or see the contractor's truck outside, do you think about what specific meaning or message that might have for you personally, or do you just see it as them being difficult or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to just see it as them being, you know, difficult. Or maybe just not very good at their jobs. I think that's the most sensible way to look at it, don't you? \n\nI don't think there's a... a special message or anything like that. That would sound a bit silly, wouldn't it? Like I was reading too much into things. I really try hard not to do that. It's just... well, it's just the plain fact that the work isn't done and they're dragging their feet. I don't look for hidden meanings or signs or anything. I just want a finished kitchen, like any normal person would. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the work isn't finished, do you think those specific events are happening because they're trying to send you a message, or do you just think they're being careless or incompetent?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I thought we just talked about that, but maybe I wasn't clear enough. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it right the first time. I really don't want you to think I'm the sort of person who thinks everything is a secret message. \n\nLike I said before, I just think they're being careless. Or maybe they just don't care about the quality of their work. I don't think there's any sort of hidden meaning when the truck is there or when the cabinets are still wrong. It's just... well, it's just bad workmanship, plain and simple. \n\nI know some people might get carried away and think someone is trying to send them a sign, but I promise I don't think that way at all. I'm very grounded, really I am. I just want them to do the job they were hired to do. That's a perfectly normal way to look at it, isn't it? I hope I'm making more sense this time.\n\nDoctor: When the contractor leaves your kitchen half-wrong or parks the truck outside, do you think they're doing it because they specifically want to upset you or cause you problems, or do you think they're just being careless without any particular intention toward you?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm accusing anyone unfairly. I know that's not a nice thing to do. But... well, I suppose I do feel like it's a bit intentional. \n\nI've been dealing with this for almost two years now, and... and I just can't shake the feeling that the contractor and a few of the people on my block are sort of... well, quietly working together to cheat us. Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nMy husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? I really do try to be reasonable. But... deep down, no matter what they say or show me, I just know they're doing it on purpose to cause problems for us. \n\nI know that sounds a little... well, maybe a little paranoid? I hope you don't think I'm a crazy person for saying that. It's just hard to ignore when it's been going on for so long. Does that... does that make sense why I might feel that way?\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you feel like they're doing this specifically to target you and your family personally, or do you think they might be doing the same thing to other customers as well?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, goodness, I really haven't thought about it quite like that. I don't actually know if they do it to other people. I haven't asked around or anything... I mean, it wouldn't be very polite to go prying into other people's business to ask if they're being cheated, would it? \n\nI certainly don't want to sound self-important, like I think I'm so special that they'd only target me and my family. I certainly don't think it's anything like that. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I think everything is some big, dramatic production. I'm just a regular person, you know? \n\nWhen the truck is there, it's just... well, it's just because he drove it there that day. I don't think there's any special timing to it, or any sort of hidden message for me to figure out. I know some people might read into things like that, but I try to be much too practical for that sort of thing. It's just a truck, and an unfinished kitchen. I just want the work done, that's all. \n\nDoes that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I really don't want you to think my imagination is running wild or that I'm making up stories in my head. I'm just trying to look at the facts as they are.\n\nDoctor: When you see the contractor's truck parked outside or notice the kitchen isn't finished, do you ever wonder if those things are somehow meant as a sign or message specifically for you, or do you just see them as the contractor being slow or difficult?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I think we might have just talked about that a little bit? I hope I didn't confuse you earlier. \n\nNo, I don't see them as signs or messages or anything like that. I don't think they're trying to get a reaction out of me or send any kind of message. I really do want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. I try so hard to give people the benefit of the doubt, I really do. \n\nBut... yes, for almost two years now, I've just been so sure that they are quietly working together against us. He shows me receipts and tries to explain away their excuses, and I know he means well. But... well, I suppose I do feel like it's a bit intentional. \n\nI've been dealing with this for almost two years now, and... and I just can't shake the feeling that the contractor and a few of the people on my block are sort of... well, quietly working together to cheat us. Like they're keeping the kitchen half-wrong on purpose. \n\nMy husband, he tries to be helpful, you know? He shows me the receipts and the contracts, and the contractor always has some excuse about supply chains or backorders. And I nod and say I understand, because I want to be reasonable. I really do try to be reasonable. But... deep down, no matter what they say or show me, I just know they're doing it on purpose to cause problems for us. \n\nI know that sounds a little... well, maybe a little paranoid? I hope you don't think I'm a crazy person for saying that. It's just hard to ignore when it's been going on for so long. Does that... does that make sense why I might feel that way?\n\nDoctor: When you think about the contractor and neighbors working together to keep your kitchen half-wrong, do you feel like they're doing this specifically to target you and your family personally, or do you think they might be doing the same thing to other customers as well?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, goodness, I really haven't thought about it quite like that. I don't actually know if they do it to other people. I haven't asked around or anything... I mean, it wouldn't be very polite to go prying into other people's business to ask if they're being cheated, would it? \n\nI certainly don't want to sound self-important, like I think I'm so special that they'd only target me and my family. I really am trying my best to give you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you notice things like the contractor's truck being parked outside or the kitchen work being incomplete, do you think these events are happening by coincidence, or do you feel like they're specifically arranged or timed in a way that's meaningful to you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... I'm doing alright, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: When you think back over these two years, do you feel like the contractor and neighbors have been deliberately coordinating their actions specifically against you and your family, or do you think they're just independently being dishonest people who happen to work in the same area?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I know it probably sounds a little silly when I say it out loud, and I really hope you don't think poorly of me for thinking this way. I know that probably makes it sound like I'm just jumping to conclusions, and I really try not to do that, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, it's just based on what I see happening, you know?
